I apologized, but… yeah, that’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Since the warehouse inspection, have you been feeling guilty about anything, even if you know it wasn’t your fault?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really guilt, exactly. I mean, I’ve wondered if I handled myself okay afterward, or if I’m making too much of it, but I don’t really feel like what happened was my fault.\n\nI hope that makes sense. I’m not trying to dodge the question—I just don’t think guilt is the main thing for me.\n\nDoctor: Since the warehouse inspection, have you found yourself feeling ongoing fear, anger, or shame, even when you're not actively thinking about the event?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’m walking around feeling afraid or angry all the time, exactly.\n\nMaybe a little on edge sometimes, but I don’t know if that counts as ongoing fear. And shame… I mean, I feel embarrassed talking about it, but I don’t think I’ve noticed a big constant feeling like that when I’m not thinking about the inspection. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any lasting feelings of fear, anger, or shame since the warehouse inspection, even when you’re not directly thinking about it?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think I’ve noticed that as a lasting thing, no. Like, if I’m reminded of it then I can feel uncomfortable, but when I’m just going through my day, I wouldn’t say I’m carrying around fear or anger or shame.\n\nAt least, not that I can clearly point to. I’m sorry if that’s not a very helpful answer.\n\nDoctor: Since the warehouse inspection, have you noticed yourself feeling detached from others or losing interest in activities you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a major way. I mean, I’ve maybe been a little quieter than usual, but I wouldn’t say I feel detached from people.\n\nAnd I’m still doing the things I normally do, more or less. Is that… is that the kind of detail you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Since the warehouse inspection, have you found yourself avoiding certain places, people, or activities that remind you of what happened?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m still going to work and everything, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just refusing to do my job. But I’ve been kind of avoiding the warehouse file specifically.\n\nAnd when emails about it come in, or industrial account calls pop up, I… I’ll let them go to voicemail more than I should. Even just opening those emails makes my stomach drop. I know that’s probably not ideal. Is that the kind of avoiding you mean?\n\nDoctor: Since the warehouse inspection, have you noticed yourself feeling more irritable, anxious, or emotionally numb than usual?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe more irritable than usual, yeah. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m taking it out on everyone, because I’m really trying not to.\n\nBut I did snap at a junior analyst over this tiny spreadsheet question, and it came out way harsher than I meant. Afterward I felt really embarrassed, because that’s not how I usually treat people. I apologized, but… yeah, that’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Since the warehouse inspection, have you been feeling guilty about anything, even if you know it wasn’t your fault?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really guilt, exactly.
